## failing check

### Context Mode > Security
## Security

Context Mode enforces the same permission rules you already use — but extends them to the MCP sandbox. If you block `sudo`, it's also blocked inside `ctx_execute`, `ctx_execute_file`, and `ctx_batch_execute`.

**Zero setup required.** If you haven't configured any permissions, nothing changes. This only activates when you add rules.

```json
{
  "permissions": {
    "deny": [
      "Bash(sudo *)",
      "Bash(rm -rf /*)",
      "Read(.env)",
      "Read(**/.env*)"
    ],
    "allow": [
      "Bash(git:*)",
      "Bash(npm:*)"
    ]
  }
}
```

Add this to your project's `.claude/settings.json` (or `~/.claude/settings.json` for global rules). All platforms read security policies from Claude Code's settings format — even on Gemini CLI, VS Code Copilot, and OpenCode. Codex CLI has no hook support, so security enforcement is not available.

The pattern is `Tool(what to match)` where `*` means "anything".

Commands chained with `&&`, `;`, or `|` are split — each part is checked separately. `echo hello && sudo rm -rf /tmp` is blocked because the `sudo` part matches the deny rule.

**deny** always wins over **allow**. More specific (project-level) rules override global ones.

### Context Mode > Session Continuity (2)
…i Agent** — High coverage. All tool lifecycle hooks (`after_tool_call`, `before_compaction`, `session_start`) fire via the native gateway plugin. User decisions aren't captured but file edits, git ops, errors, and tasks are fully tracked. Falls back to DB snapshot reconstruction if compaction hooks fail on older gateway versions. See [`docs/adapters/openclaw.md`](docs/adapters/openclaw.md).

**Codex CLI** — No session support. No hooks means no event capture. Each compaction or new session starts fresh. Requires manually copying `AGENTS.md` to your project root.

**Antigravity** — No session s…

### Context Mode > Install (3)
…mpaction recovery hooks activate automatically — no manual hook configuration or routing file needed.

> **Minimum version:** OpenClaw >2026.1.29 — this includes the `api.on()` lifecycle fix from [PR #9761](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/9761). On older versions, lifecycle hooks silently fail. The adapter falls back to DB snapshot reconstruction (less precise but preserves critical state).

Full documentation: [`docs/adapters/openclaw.md`](docs/adapters/openclaw.md)

### Context Mode > Session Continuity (2)
…odel continues without asking "what were we doing?"
- **Tasks** — checkbox format with completion status (`[x]` completed, `[ ]` pending)
- **Key Decisions** — user corrections and preferences ("use X instead", "don't do Y")
- **Files Modified** — all files touched during the session
- **Unresolved Errors** — errors that haven't been fixed
- **Git** — operations performed (checkout, commit, push, status)
- **Project Rules** — CLAUDE.md / GEMINI.md / AGENTS.md paths
- **MCP Tools Used** — tool names with call counts
- **Subagent Tasks** — delegated work summaries
- **Skills Used** — slash commands invoked…

… building compaction snapshots, and restoring state after compaction or `--continue`.

**Gemini CLI** — High coverage. PostToolUse (AfterTool), PreCompact (PreCompress), and SessionStart all fire. Missing UserPromptSubmit, so user decisions and corrections aren't captured — but file edits, git ops, errors, and tasks are fully tracked.

**VS Code Copilot** — High coverage. Same as Gemini CLI — PostToolUse, PreCompact, and SessionStart all fire. User decisions aren't captured but all tool-level events are.

…issues/14808)). Events are stored but not automatically restored after compaction.

### Context Mode > Session Continuity (3)
**Zed** — No session support. Same as Codex CLI — no hooks, no event capture. Requires manually copying `AGENTS.md` to your project root. Auto-detected via MCP protocol handshake (`clientInfo.name`).

**Kiro** — Partial coverage. Native `preToolUse` and `postToolUse` hooks capture tool events and enforce sandbox routing. `agentSpawn` (the Kiro equivalent of SessionStart) is not yet implemented, so session restore after compaction is not available. Requires manually copying `KIRO.md` to your project root. Auto-detected via MCP protocol handshake (`clientInfo.name`).

**Pi Coding Agent** — High coverage. The extension registers all key lifecycle events: `tool_call` (PreToolUse), `tool_result` (PostToolUse), `session_start` (SessionStart), and `session_before_compact` (PreCompact). File edits, git ops, errors, and tasks are fully tracked. Session restore after compaction works via the extension's event hooks.

</details>

### Context Mode > The Problem
## The Problem

Every MCP tool call dumps raw data into your context window. A Playwright snapshot costs 56 KB. Twenty GitHub issues cost 59 KB. One access log — 45 KB. After 30 minutes, 40% of your context is gone. And when the agent compacts the conversation to free space, it forgets which files it was editing, what tasks are in progress, and what you last asked for.

Context Mode is an MCP server that solves both halves of this problem:

1. **Context Saving** — Sandbox tools keep raw data out of the context window. 315 KB becomes 5.4 KB. 98% reduction.
2. **Session Continuity** — Every file edit, git operation, task, error, and user decision is tracked in SQLite. When the conversation compacts, context-mode doesn't dump this data back into context — it indexes events into FTS5 and retrieves only what's relevant via BM25 search. The model picks up exactly where you left off. If you don't `--continue`, previous session data is deleted immediately — a fresh session means a clean slate.

https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/07013dbf-07c0-4ef1-974a-33ea1207637b

## PENDING

… preserved.

After compaction, the model receives a **Session Guide** — a structured narrative with actionable sections:

- **Last Request** — user's last prompt, so the model continues without asking "what were we doing?"
- **Tasks** — checkbox format with completion status (`[x]` completed, `[ ]` pending)
- **Key Decisions** — user corrections and preferences ("use X instead", "don't do Y")
- **Files Modified** — all files touched during the session
- **Unresolved Errors** — errors that haven't been fixed
- **Git** — operations performed (checkout, commit, push, status)
- **Project Rules** —…

## claude

### claude workflow
# claude workflow

name: Claude Code

on:
  issue_comment:
    types: [created]
  pull_request_review_comment:
    types: [created]
  issues:
    types: [opened, assigned]
  pull_request_review:
    types: [submitted]

jobs:
  claude:
    if: |
      (github.event_name == 'issue_comment' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'pull_request_review_comment' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'pull_request_review' && contains(github.event.review.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'issues' && (contains(github.event.issue.body, '@claude') || contains(github.event.issue.title, '@claude')))
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pull-requests: read
      issues: read
      id-token: write
      actions: read # Required for Claude to read CI results on PRs
    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 1

      - name: Run Claude Code
        id: claude
        uses: anthropics/claude-code-action@v1
        with:
          claude_code_oauth_token: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN }}

          # This is an optional setting that allows Claude to read CI results on PRs
          additional_permissions: |
            actions: read

          # Optional: Give a custom prompt to Claude. If this is not specified, Claude will perform the instructions specified in the comment that tagged it.
          # prompt: 'Update the pull request description to include a summary of changes.'

          # Optional: Add claude_args to customize behavior and configuration
          # See https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/blob/main/docs/usage.md
          # or https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference for available options
          # claude_args: '--allowed-tools Bash(gh pr:*)'

### claude-code-review workflow
# claude-code-review workflow

name: Claude Code Review

on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize, ready_for_review, reopened]
    # Optional: Only run on specific file changes
    # paths:
    #   - "src/**/*.ts"
    #   - "src/**/*.tsx"
    #   - "src/**/*.js"
    #   - "src/**/*.jsx"

jobs:
  claude-review:
    # Optional: Filter by PR author
    # if: |
    #   github.event.pull_request.user.login == 'external-contributor' ||
    #   github.event.pull_request.user.login == 'new-developer' ||
    #   github.event.pull_request.author_association == 'FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR'

    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pull-requests: read
      issues: read
      id-token: write

    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 1

      - name: Run Claude Code Review
        id: claude-review
        uses: anthropics/claude-code-action@v1
        with:
          claude_code_oauth_token: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN }}
          plugin_marketplaces: 'https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code.git'
          plugins: 'code-review@claude-code-plugins'
          prompt: '/code-review:code-review ${{ github.repository }}/pull/${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}'
          # See https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/blob/main/docs/usage.md
          # or https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference for available options

### pr-agent workflow
# pr-agent workflow

name: AI Code Review (PR-Agent)

on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize, reopened]
  issue_comment:
    types: [created]

permissions:
  contents: read
  pull-requests: write
  issues: write

jobs:
  pr-agent:
    if: >
      github.event_name == 'pull_request' ||
      (github.event_name == 'issue_comment' &&
       contains(github.event.comment.body, '/review') &&
       github.event.issue.pull_request)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    name: PR-Agent Review
    steps:
      - name: PR-Agent Auto Review
        uses: qodo-ai/pr-agent@main
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          CONFIG.MODEL: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"
          CONFIG.MODEL_TURBO: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"
          CONFIG.FALLBACK_MODELS: '["anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"]'
          ANTHROPIC.KEY: ${{ secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }}
          PR_REVIEWER.REQUIRE_FOCUSED_REVIEW: "true"
          PR_REVIEWER.REQUIRE_SECURITY_REVIEW: "true"
          PR_REVIEWER.EXTRA_INSTRUCTIONS: >
            Focus on: security vulnerabilities (SQL injection, XSS, auth bypass),
            AGPL/copyleft license violations, proper error handling, and Go/Python
            idioms. Flag any hardcoded secrets.
          PR_CODE_SUGGESTIONS.EXTRA_INSTRUCTIONS: >
            Prefer idiomatic Go patterns. For Python, ensure type hints and
            proper async handling. Check for RLS policy gaps in SQL migrations.
        with:
          command: auto_review

## dependency-review

### security workflow
# security workflow

name: Security Scanning

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - '**.go'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
      - 'ai-worker/**'
      - 'Dockerfile'
      - 'ai-worker/Dockerfile'
      - '.github/workflows/security.yml'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - '**.go'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
      - 'ai-worker/**'
      - 'Dockerfile'
      - 'ai-worker/Dockerfile'
      - '.github/workflows/security.yml'
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 6 * * 1'  # Weekly on Monday at 06:00 UTC

permissions:
  contents: read
  security-events: write

jobs:
  trivy-scan:
    name: Trivy Filesystem Scan
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Run Trivy vulnerability scanner
        uses: aquasecurity/trivy-action@v0.35.0
        with:
          scan-type: fs
          scan-ref: .
          severity: CRITICAL,HIGH
          exit-code: 1
          format: table

  go-vuln:
    name: Go Vulnerability Check
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Go
        uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26'
          cache: true

      - name: Install govulncheck
        run: go install golang.org/x/vuln/cmd/govulncheck@v1.1.4

      - name: Run govulncheck
        run: govulncheck ./...

  dependency-review:
    name: Dependency Review
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Dependency Review
        uses: actions/dependency-review-action@v4
        with:
          fail-on-severity: critical
          deny-licenses: AGPL-3.0, SSPL-1.0

### go workflow
# go workflow

name: Go CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
      - '.github/workflows/go.yml'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
      - '.github/workflows/go.yml'

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  build-and-test:
    name: Build & Test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Go
        uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26'
          cache: true

      - name: Download dependencies
        run: go mod download

      - name: Build
        run: go build ./cmd/api

      - name: Test
        run: go test -race -short -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...

      - name: Upload coverage to Codecov
        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v5
        with:
          files: coverage.txt
          fail_ci_if_error: false

      - name: Vet
        run: go vet ./...

  lint:
    name: Lint
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Go
        uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26'
          cache: true

      - name: Run golangci-lint
        uses: golangci/golangci-lint-action@v9
        with:
          version: v2.11.4

### pr-agent workflow
# pr-agent workflow

name: AI Code Review (PR-Agent)

on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize, reopened]
  issue_comment:
    types: [created]

permissions:
  contents: read
  pull-requests: write
  issues: write

jobs:
  pr-agent:
    if: >
      github.event_name == 'pull_request' ||
      (github.event_name == 'issue_comment' &&
       contains(github.event.comment.body, '/review') &&
       github.event.issue.pull_request)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    name: PR-Agent Review
    steps:
      - name: PR-Agent Auto Review
        uses: qodo-ai/pr-agent@main
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          CONFIG.MODEL: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"
          CONFIG.MODEL_TURBO: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"
          CONFIG.FALLBACK_MODELS: '["anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"]'
          ANTHROPIC.KEY: ${{ secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }}
          PR_REVIEWER.REQUIRE_FOCUSED_REVIEW: "true"
          PR_REVIEWER.REQUIRE_SECURITY_REVIEW: "true"
          PR_REVIEWER.EXTRA_INSTRUCTIONS: >
            Focus on: security vulnerabilities (SQL injection, XSS, auth bypass),
            AGPL/copyleft license violations, proper error handling, and Go/Python
            idioms. Flag any hardcoded secrets.
          PR_CODE_SUGGESTIONS.EXTRA_INSTRUCTIONS: >
            Prefer idiomatic Go patterns. For Python, ensure type hints and
            proper async handling. Check for RLS policy gaps in SQL migrations.
        with:
          command: auto_review

### claude workflow
# claude workflow

name: Claude Code

on:
  issue_comment:
    types: [created]
  pull_request_review_comment:
    types: [created]
  issues:
    types: [opened, assigned]
  pull_request_review:
    types: [submitted]

jobs:
  claude:
    if: |
      (github.event_name == 'issue_comment' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'pull_request_review_comment' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'pull_request_review' && contains(github.event.review.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'issues' && (contains(github.event.issue.body, '@claude') || contains(github.event.issue.title, '@claude')))
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pull-requests: read
      issues: read
      id-token: write
      actions: read # Required for Claude to read CI results on PRs
    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 1

      - name: Run Claude Code
        id: claude
        uses: anthropics/claude-code-action@v1
        with:
          claude_code_oauth_token: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN }}

          # This is an optional setting that allows Claude to read CI results on PRs
          additional_permissions: |
            actions: read

          # Optional: Give a custom prompt to Claude. If this is not specified, Claude will perform the instructions specified in the comment that tagged it.
          # prompt: 'Update the pull request description to include a summary of changes.'

          # Optional: Add claude_args to customize behavior and configuration
          # See https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/blob/main/docs/usage.md
          # or https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference for available options
          # claude_args: '--allowed-tools Bash(gh pr:*)'

### Context Mode > Session Continuity (1) _(source: batch:Repo info,PR 100 detail,PR 101 detail,PR 102 detail,PR 103 detail,PR 104 detail)_
…ession continuity (capture + snapshot + restore) works on **Claude Code**, **Gemini CLI**, **VS Code Copilot**, and **OpenCode**. **Cursor** captures tool events via `preToolUse`/`postToolUse`, but `sessionStart` is currently rejected by Cursor's validator ([forum report](https://forum.cursor.com/t/unknown-hook-type-sessionstart/149566)), so session restore after compaction is not available yet. **OpenCode** uses the `experimental.session.compacting` plugin hook for compaction recovery, but SessionStart is not yet available ([#14808](https://github.com/sst/opencode/issues/14808)), so startup/r…

### Context Mode > Install (2) _(source: batch:Repo info,PR 100 detail,PR 101 detail,PR 102 detail,PR 103 detail,PR 104 detail)_
…tats`.

**Routing:** Hooks enforce routing programmatically via `preToolUse`/`postToolUse`. The `.cursor/rules/context-mode.mdc` file provides routing instructions at session start since Cursor's `sessionStart` hook is currently rejected by their validator ([forum report](https://forum.cursor.com/t/unknown-hook-type-sessionstart/149566)). Project `.cursor/hooks.json` overrides `~/.cursor/hooks.json`.

### Context Mode > Install (4) _(source: batch:Repo info,PR 100 detail,PR 101 detail,PR 102 detail,PR 103 detail,PR 104 detail)_
**Verify:** Start a session and type `ctx stats`. Context-mode tools should appear and respond.

**Routing:** Manual. The `AGENTS.md` file is the only enforcement method (~60% compliance). There is no programmatic interception — the model can run raw `curl`, read large files, or bypass sandbox tools at any time. Hook support PRs [#2904](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/2904) and [#9796](https://github.com/openai/codex/pull/9796) were closed without merge.

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Antigravity</strong> — MCP-only, no hooks</summary>

**Prerequisites:** Node.js 18+, Antigravity installed.

**Install:**

1. Install context-mode globally:

   ```bash
   npm install -g context-mode
   ```

2. Add to `~/.gemini/antigravity/mcp_config.json`:

   ```json
   {
     "mcpServers": {
       "context-mode": {
         "command": "context-mode"
       }
     }
   }
   ```

3. Copy routing instructions (Antigravity has no hook support):

   ```bash
   cp node_modules/context-mode/configs/antigravity/GEMINI.md ./GEMINI.md
   ```

4. Restart Antigravity.

**Verify:** In an Antigravity session, type `ctx stats`. Context-mode tools should appear and respond.

**Routing:** Manual. The `GEMINI.md` file is the only enforcement method (~60% compliance). There is no programmatic interception. Auto-detected via MCP protocol handshake (`clientInfo.name`) — no manual platform configuration needed.

Full configs: [`c…

…figs/antigravity/mcp_config.json) | [`configs/antigravity/GEMINI.md`](configs/antigravity/GEMINI.md)

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Kiro</strong> — hooks with steering file</summary>

**Prerequisites:** Node.js 18+, Kiro with MCP enabled (Settings > search "MCP").

**Install:**

1. Install context-mode globally:

   ```bash
   npm install -g context-mode
   ```

2. Add to `.kiro/settings/mcp.json` in your project (or `~/.kiro/settings/mcp.json` for global):

   ```json
   {
     "mcpServers": {
       "context-mode": {
         "command": "context-mode"
       }
     }
   }
   ```

3. Create `.kiro/hooks/context-mode.json`:

   ```json
   {
     "name": "context-mode",
     "description": "Context-mode hooks for context window protection",
     "hooks": {
       "preToolUse": [
         { "matcher": "*", "command": "context-mode hook kiro pretooluse" }
       ],
       "postToolUse": [
         { "matcher": "*", "command": "context-mode hook kiro posttooluse" }
       ]
     }
   }
   ```

4. Copy routing instructions. Kiro's `agentSpawn` (SessionStart) is not yet implemented, so the model needs a routing file at session start:

   ```bash
   cp node_modules/context-mode/configs/kiro/KIRO.md ./KIRO.md
   ```

5. Restart Kiro.

**Verify:** Open the Kiro panel > MCP Servers tab and confirm "context-mode" shows a green status indicator. In chat, type `ctx stats`.

**Routing:** Hooks enforce routing programmatically via `preToolUse`/`postToolUse`. The `KIRO.md` file provides routing instructions since `agentSpawn` (SessionStart equivalent)

### Context Mode > Install (2) _(source: batch:Repo info,PR 100 detail,PR 101 detail,PR 102 detail,PR 103 detail,PR 104 detail)_
> **Why the BeforeTool matcher?** It targets only tools that produce large output (`run_shell_command`, `read_file`, `read_many_files`, `grep_search`, `search_file_content`, `web_fetch`, `activate_skill`) plus context-mode's own tools (`mcp__plugin_context-mode`). This avoids unnecessary hook overhead on lightweight tools while intercepting every tool that could flood your context window.

Full config reference: [`configs/gemini-cli/settings.json`](configs/gemini-cli/settings.json)

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>VS Code Copilot</strong> — hooks with SessionStart</summary>

**Prerequisites:** Node.js 18+, VS Code with Copilot Chat v0.32+.

**Install:**

1. Install context-mode globally:

   ```bash
   npm install -g context-mode
   ```

2. Create `.vscode/mcp.json` in your project root:

   ```json
   {
     "servers": {
       "context-mode": {
         "command": "context-mode"
       }
     }
   }
   ```

3. Create `.github/hooks/context-mode.json`:

   ```json
   {
     "hooks": {
       "PreToolUse": [
         { "type": "command", "command": "context-mode hook vscode-copilot pretooluse" }
       ],
       "PostToolUse": [
         { "type": "command", "command": "context-mode hook vscode-copilot posttooluse" }
       ],
       "SessionStart": [
         { "type": "command", "command": "context-mode hook vscode-copilot sessionstart" }
       ]
     }
   }
   ```

4. Restart VS Code.

**Verify:** Open Copilot Chat and type `ctx stats`. Context-mode tools should appear and respond.

**Routing:** Automatic. The SessionStart hook injects routing instructions at runtime — no `copilot-instructions.md` file is written to your project.

Full hook config including PreCompact: [`configs/vscode-copilot/hooks.json`](configs/vscode-copilot/hooks.json)

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Cursor</strong> — hooks without SessionStart</summary>

**Prerequisites:** Node.js 18+, Cursor with agent mode.

**Install:**

1. Install context-mode globally:

   ```bash
   npm install -g context-mode
   ```

2. Create `.cursor/mcp.json` in your project root (or `~/.cursor/mcp.json` for global):

   ```json
   {
     "mcpServers": {
       "context-mode": {
         "command": "context-mode"
       }
     }
   }
   ```

3. Create `.cursor/hooks.json` (or `~/.cursor/hooks.json` for global):

   ```json
   {
     "version": 1,
     "hooks": {
       "preToolUse": [
         {
           "command": "context-mode hook cursor pretooluse",
           "matcher": "Shell|Read|Grep|WebFetch|Task|MCP:ctx_execute|MCP:ctx_execute_file|MCP:ctx_batch_execute"
         }
       ],
       "postToolUse": [
         {
           "command": "context-mode hook cursor posttooluse"
         }
       ]
     }
   }
   ```

   The `preToolUse` matcher is optional — without it, the hook fires on all tools.

4. Copy the routing rules file. Cursor lacks a SessionStart hook, so the model needs a rules file for routing awareness:

   ```bash
   mkdir -p .cursor/rules
   cp node_modules/c

### claude workflow _(source: batch:claude workflow,claude-code-review workflow,pr-agent workflow,go workflow,securi)_
# claude workflow

name: Claude Code

on:
  issue_comment:
    types: [created]
  pull_request_review_comment:
    types: [created]
  issues:
    types: [opened, assigned]
  pull_request_review:
    types: [submitted]

jobs:
  claude:
    if: |
      (github.event_name == 'issue_comment' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'pull_request_review_comment' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'pull_request_review' && contains(github.event.review.body, '@claude')) ||
      (github.event_name == 'issues' && (contains(github.event.issue.body, '@claude') || contains(github.event.issue.title, '@claude')))
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pull-requests: read
      issues: read
      id-token: write
      actions: read # Required for Claude to read CI results on PRs
    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 1

      - name: Run Claude Code
        id: claude
        uses: anthropics/claude-code-action@v1
        with:
          claude_code_oauth_token: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN }}

          # This is an optional setting that allows Claude to read CI results on PRs
          additional_permissions: |
            actions: read

          # Optional: Give a custom prompt to Claude. If this is not specified, Claude will perform the instructions specified in the comment that tagged it.
          # prompt: 'Update the pull request description to include a summary of changes.'

          # Optional: Add claude_args to customize behavior and configuration
          # See https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/blob/main/docs/usage.md
          # or https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference for available options
          # claude_args: '--allowed-tools Bash(gh pr:*)'

### claude-code-review workflow _(source: batch:claude workflow,claude-code-review workflow,pr-agent workflow,go workflow,securi)_
# claude-code-review workflow

name: Claude Code Review

on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize, ready_for_review, reopened]
    # Optional: Only run on specific file changes
    # paths:
    #   - "src/**/*.ts"
    #   - "src/**/*.tsx"
    #   - "src/**/*.js"
    #   - "src/**/*.jsx"

jobs:
  claude-review:
    # Optional: Filter by PR author
    # if: |
    #   github.event.pull_request.user.login == 'external-contributor' ||
    #   github.event.pull_request.user.login == 'new-developer' ||
    #   github.event.pull_request.author_association == 'FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR'

    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pull-requests: read
      issues: read
      id-token: write

    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 1

      - name: Run Claude Code Review
        id: claude-review
        uses: anthropics/claude-code-action@v1
        with:
          claude_code_oauth_token: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN }}
          plugin_marketplaces: 'https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code.git'
          plugins: 'code-review@claude-code-plugins'
          prompt: '/code-review:code-review ${{ github.repository }}/pull/${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}'
          # See https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/blob/main/docs/usage.md
          # or https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference for available options

### pr-agent workflow _(source: batch:claude workflow,claude-code-review workflow,pr-agent workflow,go workflow,securi)_
# pr-agent workflow

name: AI Code Review (PR-Agent)

on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize, reopened]
  issue_comment:
    types: [created]

permissions:
  contents: read
  pull-requests: write
  issues: write

jobs:
  pr-agent:
    if: >
      github.event_name == 'pull_request' ||
      (github.event_name == 'issue_comment' &&
       contains(github.event.comment.body, '/review') &&
       github.event.issue.pull_request)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    name: PR-Agent Review
    steps:
      - name: PR-Agent Auto Review
        uses: qodo-ai/pr-agent@main
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          CONFIG.MODEL: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"
          CONFIG.MODEL_TURBO: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"
          CONFIG.FALLBACK_MODELS: '["anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514"]'
          ANTHROPIC.KEY: ${{ secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }}
          PR_REVIEWER.REQUIRE_FOCUSED_REVIEW: "true"
          PR_REVIEWER.REQUIRE_SECURITY_REVIEW: "true"
          PR_REVIEWER.EXTRA_INSTRUCTIONS: >
            Focus on: security vulnerabilities (SQL injection, XSS, auth bypass),
            AGPL/copyleft license violations, proper error handling, and Go/Python
            idioms. Flag any hardcoded secrets.
          PR_CODE_SUGGESTIONS.EXTRA_INSTRUCTIONS: >
            Prefer idiomatic Go patterns. For Python, ensure type hints and
            proper async handling. Check for RLS policy gaps in SQL migrations.
        with:
          command: auto_review

### Context Mode > Security _(source: batch:Repo info,PR 100 detail,PR 101 detail,PR 102 detail,PR 103 detail,PR 104 detail)_
## Security

Context Mode enforces the same permission rules you already use — but extends them to the MCP sandbox. If you block `sudo`, it's also blocked inside `ctx_execute`, `ctx_execute_file`, and `ctx_batch_execute`.

**Zero setup required.** If you haven't configured any permissions, nothing changes. This only activates when you add rules.

```json
{
  "permissions": {
    "deny": [
      "Bash(sudo *)",
      "Bash(rm -rf /*)",
      "Read(.env)",
      "Read(**/.env*)"
    ],
    "allow": [
      "Bash(git:*)",
      "Bash(npm:*)"
    ]
  }
}
```

Add this to your project's `.claude/settings.json` (or `~/.claude/settings.json` for global rules). All platforms read security policies from Claude Code's settings format — even on Gemini CLI, VS Code Copilot, and OpenCode. Codex CLI has no hook support, so security enforcement is not available.

The pattern is `Tool(what to match)` where `*` means "anything".

Commands chained with `&&`, `;`, or `|` are split — each part is checked separately. `echo hello && sudo rm -rf /tmp` is blocked because the `sudo` part matches the deny rule.

**deny** always wins over **allow**. More specific (project-level) rules override global ones.

### go workflow _(source: batch:claude workflow,claude-code-review workflow,pr-agent workflow,go workflow,securi)_
# go workflow

name: Go CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
      - '.github/workflows/go.yml'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
      - '.github/workflows/go.yml'

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  build-and-test:
    name: Build & Test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Go
        uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26'
          cache: true

      - name: Download dependencies
        run: go mod download

      - name: Build
        run: go build ./cmd/api

      - name: Test
        run: go test -race -short -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...

      - name: Upload coverage to Codecov
        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v5
        with:
          files: coverage.txt
          fail_ci_if_error: false

      - name: Vet
        run: go vet ./...

  lint:
    name: Lint
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Go
        uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26'
          cache: true

      - name: Run golangci-lint
        uses: golangci/golangci-lint-action@v9
        with:
          version: v2.11.4

…t, so the model continues without asking "what were we doing?"

- **Unresolved Errors** — errors that haven't been fixed
- **Git** — operations performed (checkout, commit, push, status)

Options:\n   a) Remove the bypass entirely if it's not needed\n   b) If needed, validate the API key against a configured list/env var\n   Read the code carefully - if it's checking `Authorization: ApiKey <key>` with a hardcoded/unchecked value, remove or fix it.\n\n4. **Line ~193** - Add audience (`aud`) claim validation to JWT parsing. When calling `keyfunc.Parse` or the JWT library, add validation that the `aud` claim matches the expected audience (from config or env var).\n\n5. **Line ~206** - Add `signature invalid` to the JWKS refresh predicate. When the JWT signature is invalid, it should trigger a JWKS key refresh (in case keys rotated). Add this error case to whatever refresh condition is currently checked.\n\n**Also fix:**\n6. **`.github/workflows/go.yml`** - Remove `-short` flag from `go test -race -short -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...`\n7. **`.github/workflows/security.yml`** - If there's a `continue-on-error: true` on dependency-review, remove it\n\n**Steps:**\n1. Based on my analysis of all review comments on PR #102 and comparison with the current state of the files, here is the precise list of every actionable issue that still needs to be addressed:

---

## UNADDRESSED ACTIONABLE ISSUES ON PR #102

### 1. **.github/workflows/pages.yml - Line 50: VITE_API_BASE_URL → VITE_API_URL**
**Status:** ALREADY FIXED (addressed in commit 2abe62e)
- **Issue:** Workflow exported `VITE_API_BASE_URL` but frontend's `client.ts` line 1 expects `VITE_API_URL`
- **Current state:** Line 50 now correctly reads `VITE_API_URL: ${{ vars.VITE_API_URL || 'https://api.ravencloak.org' }}`
- **Result:** No action needed - already resolved

---

### 2. **.github/workflows/pages.yml - Lines 9-13: Pull Request Trigger Missing Workflow Paths**
**Status:** UNADDRESSED - ACTIONABLE ISSUE
- **File location:** `.github/workflows/pages.yml`
- **Exact problem:** PR trigger (lines 9-13) only includes `frontend/**` in its paths array, so PRs that modify the workflow file itself are not running validation
- **What needs fixing:** Add workflow file pattern to PR trigger paths array:
  ```yaml
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - "frontend/**"
      - ".github/workflows/pages.yml"  # ← ADD THIS LINE
  ```
- **Why:** Workflow-only edits should be validated via CI checks

---

### 3. **.github/workflows/pages.yml - Lines 53-62: Deploy Step Uses Invalid Secrets Context in If Condition**
**Status:** UNADDRESSED - ACTIONABLE ISSUE (2 related problems)
- **File location:** `.github/workflows/pages.yml`
- **Problem A (Line 55-56):** Env variable `HAS_CF_TOKEN` references `secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN != ''` which doesn't work as intended in GitHub Actions. The if condition allows deploys on PRs when token is missing.
- **Problem B (Lines 53-62):** Deploy step needs gated behavior: only deploy on push to main with token present, never deploy on PRs even if token exists.
- **What needs fixing:** 
  - Set env variable from secret (not reference secret in if)
  - Change if condition to require push event, main branch, AND token presence
  - Example fix:
  ```yaml
  - name: Deploy to Cloudflare Pages
    env:
      CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
    if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' && env.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN != ''
    uses: cloudflare/wrangler-action@v3
    with:
      apiToken: ${{ env.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
      accountId: ${{ vars.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}
      command: pages deploy frontend/dist --project-name=raven-frontend --commit-dirty=true
      gitHubToken: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
  ```

---

### 4. **.github/workflows/go.yml - Line 47: -short Flag Skips Integration Tests**
**Status:** UNADDRESSED - ACTIONABLE ISSUE
- **File location:** `.github/workflows/go.yml`
- **Exact problem:** Line 47 uses `go test -race -short -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...` which causes tests checking `testing.Short()` (like migrations_test.go) to be skipped
- **What needs fixing:** Remove the `-short` flag from the test command:
  ```yaml
  - name: Test
    run: go test -race -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...
  ```
- **Why:** Integration tests like migrations_test.go need to run in CI to validate schema/migration integrity

---

### 5. **.github/workflows/security.yml - Missing Dependency Review Job**
**Status:** CHANGED (job was removed from the workflow entirely)
- **Issue context:** CodeRabbit previously flagged that the dependency-review job had `continue-on-error: true` (line 71), which disabled the security gate
- **Current state:** The entire dependency-review job has been removed from security.yml (verified: lines 1-67 show only trivy-scan and go-vuln jobs)
- **Assessment:** This is a design decision (removing the job vs fixing it). Not an unaddressed issue if intentional, but note: security gate for dependency vulnerabilities is now inactive.

---

### 6. **migrations/migrations_test.go - Line 278: rows.Close() Error Silently Discarded**
**Status:** PARTIALLY FIXED but INCOMPLETE - ACTIONABLE ISSUE REMAINS
- **File location:** `migrations/migrations_test.go`
- **Current state (Line 278):** `defer func() { _ = rows.Close() }()`
- **Problem:** Still silently discards rows.Close() errors with `_` instead of handling them

```
     1→package middleware
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"errors"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"log"
     8→	"net/http"
     9→	"strings"
    10→	"sync"
    11→	"time"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/MicahParks/keyfunc/v3"
    14→	"github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
    15→	"github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5"
    16→
    17→	"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/config"
    18→)
    19→
    20→// contextKey is a private type for context keys to avoid collisions with other packages.
    21→type contextKey string
    22→
    23→const (
    24→	// ContextKeyUserID is the context key for the JWT subject (user ID).
    25→	ContextKeyUserID contextKey = "user_id"
    26→	// ContextKeyOrgID is the context key for the organisation ID claim.
    27→	ContextKeyOrgID contextKey = "org_id"
    28→	// ContextKeyOrgRole is the context key for the organisation role claim.
    29→	ContextKeyOrgRole contextKey = "org_role"
    30→	// ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs is the context key for the workspace IDs claim.
    31→	ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs contextKey = "workspace_ids"
    32→	// ContextKeyKBPermissions is the context key for the knowledge-base permissions claim.
    33→	ContextKeyKBPermissions contextKey = "kb_permissions"
    34→	// ContextKeyEmail is the context key for the user email claim.
    35→	ContextKeyEmail contextKey = "email"
    36→	// ContextKeyClaims is the context key for the full parsed Claims struct.
    37→	ContextKeyClaims contextKey = "claims"
    38→
    39→	jwksCacheTTL = time.Hour
    40→)
    41→
    42→// Claims holds the standard JWT registered claims plus custom Raven/Keycloak claims.
    43→type Claims struct {
    44→	jwt.RegisteredClaims
    45→
    46→	// Custom Keycloak / Raven claims.
    47→	OrgID          string   `json:"org_id"`
    48→	OrgRole        string   `json:"org_role"`
    49→	WorkspaceIDs   []string `json:"workspace_ids"`
    50→	KBPermissions  []string `json:"kb_permissions"`
    51→	Email          string   `json:"email"`
    52→}
    53→
    54→// authError represents a structured 401 response body.
    55→type authError struct {
    56→	Error string `json:"error"`
    57→}
    58→
    59→// jwksCache wraps a keyfunc.Keyfunc with TTL-based refresh logic.
    60→type jwksCache struct {
    61→	mu          sync.RWMutex
    62→	keyfunc     keyfunc.Keyfunc
    63→	lastRefresh time.Time
    64→	jwksURL     string
    65→}
    66→
    67→// newJWKSCache creates a new cache and performs an initial fetch.
    68→func newJWKSCache(jwksURL string) (*jwksCache, error) {
    69→	c := &jwksCache{jwksURL: jwksURL}
    70→	if err := c.refresh(); err != nil {
    71→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("initial JWKS fetch failed: %w", err)
    72→	}
    73→	return c, nil
    74→}
    75→
    76→// refresh fetches the JWKS from the remote endpoint and replaces the cached keyfunc.
    77→// A 10-second timeout is applied to the fetch to prevent indefinite blocking.
    78→func (c *jwksCache) refresh() error {
    79→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
    80→	defer cancel()
    81→
    82→	kf, err := keyfunc.NewDefaultCtx(ctx, []string{c.jwksURL})
    83→	if err != nil {
    84→		return err
    85→	}
    86→	c.mu.Lock()
    87→	c.keyfunc = kf
    88→	c.lastRefresh = time.Now()
    89→	c.mu.Unlock()
    90→	return nil
    91→}
    92→
    93→// keyFunc returns the jwt.Keyfunc, refreshing the cache if TTL has elapsed or
    94→// if forceRefresh is true.
    95→func (c *jwksCache) keyFunc(forceRefresh bool) jwt.Keyfunc {
    96→	c.mu.RLock()
    97→	expired := time.Since(c.lastRefresh) > jwksCacheTTL
    98→	kf := c.keyfunc
    99→	c.mu.RUnlock()
   100→
   101→	if forceRefresh || expired {
   102→		// Refresh in place; ignore error — continue with stale keys.
   103→		_ = c.refresh()
   104→		c.mu.RLock()
   105→		kf = c.keyfunc
   106→		c.mu.RUnlock()
   107→	}
   108→
   109→	return kf.Keyfunc
   110→}
   111→
   112→// JWTMiddleware returns a Gin handler that validates Bearer JWTs against the
   113→// Keycloak JWKS endpoint, or stubs through API-key requests.
   114→//
   115→// The middleware is intended to be applied per route-group, not globally.
   116→//
   117→// On success, the following values are stored in the Gin context:
   118→//
   119→//	ContextKeyUserID        → string (JWT sub)
   120→//	ContextKeyOrgID         → string
   121→//	ContextKeyOrgRole       → string
   122→//	ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs  → []string
   123→//	ContextKeyKBPermissions → []string
   124→//	ContextKeyEmail         → string
   125→//	ContextKeyClaims        → *Claims
   126→//
   127→// NOTE: RLS enforcement (`SET LOCAL app.current_org_id`) must be applied by
   128→// the repository layer after retrieving ContextKeyOrgID from the context,
   129→// because the DB connection is not accessible here.
   130→func JWTMiddleware(cfg *config.KeycloakConfig) gin.HandlerFunc {
   131→	jwksURL := cfg.IssuerURL + "/protocol/openid-connect/certs"
   132→
   133→	cache, err := newJWKSCache(jwksURL)
   134→	if err != nil {
   135→		// If JWKS is unavailable at startup, log a clear warning and serve 503 on
   136→		// every protected request. This typically means Keycloak is not reachable.
   137→		log.Printf("[WARN] JWT middleware: initial JWKS fetch from %s failed: %v — all protected endpoints will return 503 until the service restarts with a reachable Keycloak instance", jwksURL, err)
   138→		return func(c *gin.Context) {
   139→			c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusServiceUnavailable, authError{Error: "jwks_unavailable"})
   140→		}
   141→	}
   142→
   143→	return func(c *gin.Context) {
   144→		// --- Detect auth method ---
   145→		if apiKey := c.GetHeader("X-API-Key"); apiKey != "" {
   146→			handleAPIKey(c, apiKey)
   147→			return
   148→		}
   149→
   150→		authHeader := c.GetHeader("Authorization")
   151→		if authHeader == "" {
   152→			abortUnauthorized(c, "missing_token")
   153→			return
   154→		}
   155→
   156→		parts := strings.SplitN(authHeader, " ", 2)
   157→		if len(parts) != 2 || !strings.EqualFold(parts[0], "Bearer") {
   158→			abortUnauthorized(c, "missing_token")
   159→			return
   160→		}
   161→		rawToken := parts[1]
   162→
   163→		// --- Parse & validate JWT ---
   164→		claims, err := parseJWT(rawToken, cfg.IssuerURL, cfg.Audience, cache, false)
   165→		if err != nil {
   166→			// On any key-related error, retry once with a forced JWKS refresh
   167→			// (handles key rotation / cache staleness).
   168→			if isKeyError(err) {
   169→				claims, err = parseJWT(rawToken, cfg.IssuerURL, cfg.Audience, cache, true)
   170→			}
   171→			if err != nil {
   172→				abortWithTokenError(c, err)
   173→				return
   174→			}
   175→		}
   176→
   177→		// --- Store claims in Gin context ---
   178→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyUserID), claims.Subject)
   179→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyOrgID), claims.OrgID)
   180→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyOrgRole), claims.OrgRole)
   181→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs), claims.WorkspaceIDs)
   182→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyKBPermissions), claims.KBPermissions)
   183→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyEmail), claims.Email)
   184→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyClaims), claims)
   185→
   186→		c.Next()
   187→	}
   188→}
   189→
   190→// parseJWT validates the raw token string and returns the parsed Claims.
   191→// It validates the issuer, audience, and expiry claims in addition to the signature.
   192→func parseJWT(rawToken, issuerURL, audience string, cache *jwksCache, forceRefresh bool) (*Claims, error) {
   193→	claims := &Claims{}
   194→	_, err := jwt.ParseWithClaims(
   195→		rawToken,
   196→		claims,
   197→		cache.keyFunc(forceRefresh),
   198→		jwt.WithIssuer(issuerURL),
   199→		jwt.WithAudience(audience),
   200→		jwt.WithExpirationRequired(),
   201→		jwt.WithLeeway(5*time.Second),
   202→	)
   203→	if err != nil {
   204→		return nil, err
   205→	}
   206→	return claims, nil
   207→}
   208→
   209→// isKeyError reports whether the error is likely caused by an unknown/unresolvable
   210→// key or a stale key (suggesting a JWKS rotation rather than a malformed token).
   211→// Signature-invalid errors are included because a key rotation can produce them
   212→// when the cached JWKS is stale.
   213→func isKeyError(err error) bool {
   214→	return strings.Contains(err.Error(), "unable to find") ||
   215→		strings.Contains(err.Error(), "key") ||
   216→		strings.Contains(err.Error(), "signature") ||
   217→		errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrTokenUnverifiable) ||
   218→		errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrTokenSignatureInvalid)
   219→}
   220→
   221→// abortWithTokenError maps jwt parse errors to structured 401 responses.
   222→func abortWithTokenError(c *gin.Context, err error) {
   223→	switch {
   224→	case errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrTokenExpired):
   225→		abortUnauthorized(c, "token_expired")
   226→	default:
   227→		abortUnauthorized(c, "invalid_token")
   228→	}
   229→}
   230→
   231→// abortUnauthorized aborts the request with a structured 401 JSON body.
   232→func abortUnauthorized(c *gin.Context, code string) {
   233→	c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusUnauthorized, authError{Error: code})
   234→}
   235→
   236→// handleAPIKey stubs the API-key authentication path.
   237→// The lookup against the database is not yet implemented; a placeholder
   238→// Claims struct is stored in the context so downstream handlers can read it.
   239→//
   240→// WARNING: This stub accepts ANY non-empty X-API-Key value without validation.

   243→// TODO(issue-24): replace stub with real DB lookup and key hashing.

    26→// rsaKeyPair holds an RSA key pair and its JWK representation for tests.
    27→type rsaKeyPair struct {
    28→	private *rsa.PrivateKey
    29→	keyID   string

    31→
    32→// newTestKeyPair generates a 2048-bit RSA key pair for use in tests.
    33→func newTestKeyPair(t *testing.T) *rsaKeyPair {
    34→	t.Helper()
    35→	priv, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
    36→	require.NoError(t, err)
    37→	return &rsaKeyPair{private: priv, keyID: "test-key-1"}
    38→}
    39→
    40→// jwksJSON returns a JWKS document containing the public key of this pair.
    41→func (kp *rsaKeyPair) jwksJSON() []byte {
    42→	pub := &kp.private.PublicKey
    43→	n := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(pub.N.Bytes())
    44→	e := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(big.NewInt(int64(pub.E)).Bytes())
    45→
    46→	jwks := map[string]interface{}{
    47→		"keys": []map[string]interface{}{
    48→			{
    49→				"kty": "RSA",
    50→				"use": "sig",
    51→				"kid": kp.keyID,
    52→				"alg": "RS256",
    53→				"n":   n,
    54→				"e":   e,
    55→			},
    56→		},
    57→	}
    58→	b, _ := json.Marshal(jwks)
    59→	return b
    60→}
    61→
    62→// sign mints a JWT with the given claims signed by the test key.
    63→func (kp *rsaKeyPair) sign(t *testing.T, claims jwt.Claims) string {
    64→	t.Helper()
    65→	tok := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodRS256, claims)
    66→	tok.Header["kid"] = kp.keyID
    67→	raw, err := tok.SignedString(kp.private)
    68→	require.NoError(t, err)
    69→	return raw
    70→}
    71→
    72→// startJWKSServer starts an httptest server that serves a static JWKS document.
    73→// The caller is responsible for closing the server.
    74→func startJWKSServer(t *testing.T, kp *rsaKeyPair) *httptest.Server {
    75→	t.Helper()
    76→	jwksData := kp.jwksJSON()
    77→	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    78→		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
    79→		_, _ = w.Write(jwksData)
    80→	}))
    81→	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
    82→	return srv
    83→}

     1→package middleware
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"errors"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"log"
     8→	"net/http"
     9→	"strings"
    10→	"sync"
    11→	"time"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/MicahParks/keyfunc/v3"
    14→	"github.com/gin-gonic/gin"

    17→	"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/config"
    18→)
    19→
    20→// contextKey is a private type for context keys to avoid collisions with other packages.
    21→type contextKey string
    22→
    23→const (
    24→	// ContextKeyUserID is the context key for the JWT subject (user ID).
    25→	ContextKeyUserID contextKey = "user_id"
    26→	// ContextKeyOrgID is the context key for the organisation ID claim.
    27→	ContextKeyOrgID contextKey = "org_id"
    28→	// ContextKeyOrgRole is the context key for the organisation role claim.
    29→	ContextKeyOrgRole contextKey = "org_role"
    30→	// ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs is the context key for the workspace IDs claim.
    31→	ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs contextKey = "workspace_ids"
    32→	// ContextKeyKBPermissions is the context key for the knowledge-base permissions claim.
    33→	ContextKeyKBPermissions contextKey = "kb_permissions"
    34→	// ContextKeyEmail is the context key for the user email claim.
    35→	ContextKeyEmail contextKey = "email"
    36→	// ContextKeyClaims is the context key for the full parsed Claims struct.
    37→	ContextKeyClaims contextKey = "claims"
    38→
    39→	jwksCacheTTL = time.Hour
    40→)
    41→
    42→// Claims holds the standard JWT registered claims plus custom Raven/Keycloak claims.
    43→type Claims struct {
    44→	jwt.RegisteredClaims
    45→
    46→	// Custom Keycloak / Raven claims.
    47→	OrgID          string   `json:"org_id"`
    48→	OrgRole        string   `json:"org_role"`
    49→	WorkspaceIDs   []string `json:"workspace_ids"`
    50→	KBPermissions  []string `json:"kb_permissions"`
    51→	Email          string   `json:"email"`
    52→}
    53→
    54→// authError represents a structured 401 response body.
    55→type authError struct {
    56→	Error string `json:"error"`
    57→}
    58→
    59→// jwksCache wraps a keyfunc.Keyfunc with TTL-based refresh logic.
    60→type jwksCache struct {
    61→	mu          sync.RWMutex
    62→	keyfunc     keyfunc.Keyfunc
    63→	lastRefresh time.Time
    64→	jwksURL     string
    65→}
    66→
    67→// newJWKSCache creates a new cache and performs an initial fetch.
    68→func newJWKSCache(jwksURL string) (*jwksCache, error) {
    69→	c := &jwksCache{jwksURL: jwksURL}
    70→	if err := c.refresh(); err != nil {
    71→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("initial JWKS fetch failed: %w", err)
    72→	}
    73→	return c, nil
    74→}
    75→
    76→// refresh fetches the JWKS from the remote endpoint and replaces the cached keyfunc.
    77→// A 10-second timeout is applied to the fetch to prevent indefinite blocking.
    78→func (c *jwksCache) refresh() error {
    79→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
    80→	defer cancel()
    81→
    82→	kf, err := keyfunc.NewDefaultCtx(ctx, []string{c.jwksURL})
    83→	if err != nil {
    84→		return err
    85→	}
    86→	c.mu.Lock()
    87→	c.keyfunc = kf
    88→	c.lastRefresh = time.Now()
    89→	c.mu.Unlock()
    90→	return nil
    91→}
    92→
    93→// keyFunc returns the jwt.Keyfunc, refreshing the cache if TTL has elapsed or
    94→// if forceRefresh is true.
    95→func (c *jwksCache) keyFunc(forceRefresh bool) jwt.Keyfunc {
    96→	c.mu.RLock()
    97→	expired := time.Since(c.lastRefresh) > jwksCacheTTL
    98→	kf := c.keyfunc
    99→	c.mu.RUnlock()
   100→
   101→	if forceRefresh || expired {
   102→		// Refresh in place; ignore error — continue with stale keys.
   103→		_ = c.refresh()
   104→		c.mu.RLock()
   105→		kf = c.keyfunc
   106→		c.mu.RUnlock()
   107→	}
   108→
   109→	return kf.Keyfunc
   110→}
   111→
   112→// JWTMiddleware returns a Gin handler that validates Bearer JWTs against the
   113→// Keycloak JWKS endpoint, or stubs through API-key requests.
   114→//
   115→// The middleware is intended to be applied per route-group, not globally.
   116→//
   117→// On success, the following values are stored in the Gin context:
   118→//
   119→//	ContextKeyUserID        → string (JWT sub)
   120→//	ContextKeyOrgID         → string
   121→//	ContextKeyOrgRole       → string
   122→//	ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs  → []string
   123→//	ContextKeyKBPermissions → []string
   124→//	ContextKeyEmail         → string
   125→//	ContextKeyClaims        → *Claims
   126→//
   127→// NOTE: RLS enforcement (`SET LOCAL app.current_org_id`) must be applied by
   128→// the repository layer after retrieving ContextKeyOrgID from the context,
   129→// because the DB connection is not accessible here.
   130→func JWTMiddleware(cfg *config.KeycloakConfig) gin.HandlerFunc {
   131→	jwksURL := cfg.IssuerURL + "/protocol/openid-connect/certs"
   132→
   133→	cache, err := newJWKSCache(jwksURL)
   134→	if err != nil {
   135→		// If JWKS is unavailable at startup, log a clear warning and serve 503 on
   136→		// every protected request. This typically means Keycloak is not reachable.
   137→		log.Printf("[WARN] JWT middleware: initial JWKS fetch from %s failed: %v — all protected endpoints will return 503 until the service restarts with a reachable Keycloak instance", jwksURL, err)
   138→		return func(c *gin.Context) {
   139→			c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusServiceUnavailable, authError{Error: "jwks_unavailable"})
   140→		}
   141→	}
   142→
   143→	return func(c *gin.Context) {
   144→		// --- Detect auth method ---
   145→		if apiKey := c.GetHeader("X-API-Key"); apiKey != "" {
   146→			handleAPIKey(c, apiKey)
   147→			return
   148→		}
   149→
   150→		authHeader := c.GetHeader("Authorization")
   151→		if authHeader == "" {
   152→			abortUnauthorized(c, "missing_token")
   153→			return
   154→		}
   155→
   156→		parts := strings.SplitN(authHeader, " ", 2)
   157→		if len(parts) != 2 || !strings.EqualFold(parts[0], "Bearer") {
   158→			abortUnauthorized(c, "missing_token")
   159→			return
   160→		}
   161→		rawToken := parts[1]
   162→
   163→		// --- Parse & validate JWT ---
   164→		claims, err := parseJWT(rawToken, cfg.IssuerURL, cfg.Audience, cache, false)
   165→		if err != nil {
   166→			// On any key-related error, retry once with a forced JWKS refresh
   167→			// (handles key rotation / cache staleness).
   168→			if isKeyError(err) {
   169→				claims, err = parseJWT(rawToken, cfg.IssuerURL, cfg.Audience, cache, true)
   170→			}
   171→			if err != nil {
   172→				abortWithTokenError(c, err)
   173→				return
   174→			}
   175→		}
   176→
   177→		// --- Store claims in Gin context ---
   178→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyUserID), claims.Subject)
   179→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyOrgID), claims.OrgID)
   180→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyOrgRole), claims.OrgRole)
   181→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs), claims.WorkspaceIDs)
   182→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyKBPermissions), claims.KBPermissions)
   183→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyEmail), claims.Email)
   184→		c.Set(string(ContextKeyClaims), claims)
   185→
   186→		c.Next()
   187→	}
   188→}
   189→
   190→// parseJWT validates the raw token string and returns the parsed Claims.
   191→// It validates the issuer, audience, and expiry claims in addition to the signature.
   192→func parseJWT(rawToken, issuerURL, audience string, cache *jwksCache, forceRefresh bool) (*Claims, error) {
   193→	claims := &Claims{}
   194→	_, err := jwt.ParseWithClaims(
   195→		rawToken,
   196→		claims,
   197→		cache.keyFunc(forceRefresh),
   198→		jwt.WithIssuer(issuerURL),
   199→		jwt.WithAudience(audience),
   200→		jwt.WithExpirationRequired(),
   201→		jwt.WithLeeway(5*time.Second),
   202→	)
   203→	if err != nil {
   204→		return nil, err
   205→	}
   206→	return claims, nil
   207→}
   208→
   209→// isKeyError reports whether the error is likely caused by an unknown/unresolvable
   210→// key or a stale key (suggesting a JWKS rotation rather than a malformed token).
   211→// Signature-invalid errors are included because a key rotation can produce them
   212→// when the cached JWKS is stale.
   213→func isKeyError(err error) bool {
   214→	return strings.Contains(err.Error(), "unable to find") ||
   215→		strings.Contains(err.Error(), "key") ||
   216→		strings.Contains(err.Error(), "signature") ||
   217→		errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrTokenUnverifiable) ||
   218→		errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrTokenSignatureInvalid)
   219→}
   220→
   221→// abortWithTokenError maps jwt parse errors to structured 401 responses.
   222→func abortWithTokenError(c *gin.Context, err error) {
   223→	switch {
   224→	case errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrTokenExpired):
   225→		abortUnauthorized(c, "token_expired")
   226→	default:
   227→		abortUnauthorized(c, "invalid_token")
   228→	}
   229→}
   230→
   231→// abortUnauthorized aborts the request with a structured 401 JSON body.
   232→func abortUnauthorized(c *gin.Context, code string) {
   233→	c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusUnauthorized, authError{Error: code})
   234→}
   235→
   236→// handleAPIKey stubs the API-key authentication path.
   237→// The lookup against the database is not yet implemented; a placeholder
   238→// Claims struct is stored in the context so downstream handlers can read it.
   239→//
   240→// WARNING: This stub accepts ANY non-empty X-API-Key value without validation.
   241→// It MUST be replaced before production use.
   242→//
   243→// TODO(issue-24): replace stub with real DB lookup and key hashing.
   244→func handleAPIKey(c *gin.Context, _ string) {
   245→	log.Printf("[WARN] API key authentication is not yet validated — stub in use (see issue-24)")
   246→	stub := &Claims{}
   247→	stub.Subject = "api-key-subject-placeholder"
   248→
   249→	c.Set(string(ContextKeyUserID), stub.Subject)
   250→	c.Set(string(ContextKeyOrgID), stub.OrgID)
   251→	c.Set(string(ContextKeyOrgRole), stub.OrgRole)
   252→	c.Set(string(ContextKeyWorkspaceIDs), stub.WorkspaceIDs)
   253→	c.Set(string(ContextKeyKBPermissions), stub.KBPermissions)
   254→	c.Set(string(ContextKeyEmail), stub.Email)
   255→	c.Set(string(ContextKeyClaims), stub)
   256→
   257→	c.Next()
   258→}

    18→// newTestRateLimiter starts a miniredis instance and returns a RateLimiter
    19→// backed by it, along with a cleanup function.
    20→func newTestRateLimiter(t *testing.T) (*RateLimiter, *miniredis.Miniredis) {
    21→	t.Helper()
    22→	mr := miniredis.RunT(t)
    23→	client := redis.NewClient(&redis.Options{Addr: mr.Addr()})
    24→	rl := NewRateLimiter(client, nil)
    25→	return rl, mr
    26→}
    27→
    28→// newRateLimitRouter builds a minimal Gin router that applies the given
    29→// middleware and always responds 200 OK.
    30→func newRateLimitRouter(mw gin.HandlerFunc) *gin.Engine {
    31→	gin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)
    32→	r := gin.New()
    33→	r.Use(mw)
    34→	r.GET("/test", func(c *gin.Context) {
    35→		c.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{"ok": true})
    36→	})
    37→	return r
    38→}
    39→
    40→// doRequest fires a GET /test against the router and returns the recorder.
    41→func doRequest(r *gin.Engine) *httptest.ResponseRecorder {
    42→	w := httptest.NewRecorder()
    43→	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/test", nil)
    44→	r.ServeHTTP(w, req)
    45→	return w
    46→}

    50→// TestRateLimitBasic verifies that requests under the limit are admitted and
    51→// that the X-RateLimit-* headers are present on every response.
    52→func TestRateLimitBasic(t *testing.T) {
    53→	rl, _ := newTestRateLimiter(t)
    54→
    55→	mw := RateLimitMiddleware(rl, 5, func(c *gin.Context) string {
    56→		return "raven:rl:test:basic"
    57→	})
    58→	r := newRateLimitRouter(mw)
    59→
    60→	for i := 1; i <= 5; i++ {
    61→		w := doRequest(r)
    62→		if w.Code != http.StatusOK {
    63→			t.Fatalf("request %d: expected 200, got %d", i, w.Code)
    64→		}
    65→		if w.Header().Get("X-RateLimit-Limit") != "5" {
    66→			t.Errorf("request %d: X-RateLimit-Limit missing or wrong", i)
    67→		}
    68→		if w.Header().Get("X-RateLimit-Remaining") == "" {
    69→			t.Errorf("request %d: X-RateLimit-Remaining missing", i)
    70→		}
    71→		if w.Header().Get("X-RateLimit-Reset") == "" {
    72→			t.Errorf("request %d: X-RateLimit-Reset missing", i)
    73→		}
    74→	}
    75→}
    76→
    77→// TestRateLimitExceeded verifies that the (limit+1)th request is rejected with
    78→// 429 and the correct JSON body + Retry-After header.
    79→func TestRateLimitExceeded(t *testing.T) {
    80→	rl, _ := newTestRateLimiter(t)
    81→
    82→	const limit = 3
    83→	mw := RateLimitMiddleware(rl, limit, func(c *gin.Context) string {
    84→		return "raven:rl:test:exceeded"
    85→	})
    86→	r := newRateLimitRouter(mw)
    87→
    88→	// Exhaust the limit.
    89→	for i := 0; i < limit; i++ {
    90→		w := doRequest(r)
    91→		if w.Code != http.StatusOK {
    92→			t.Fatalf("request %d should succeed, got %d", i+1, w.Code)
    93→		}
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	// Next request should be rejected.
    97→	w := doRequest(r)
    98→	if w.Code != http.StatusTooManyRequests {
    99→		t.Fatalf("expected 429, got %d", w.Code)
   100→	}
   101→	if w.Header().Get("Retry-After") == "" {
   102→		t.Error("expected Retry-After header on 429 response")
   103→	}
   104→
   105→	var body map[string]interface{}
   106→	if err := json.NewDecoder(w.Body).Decode(&body); err != nil {
   107→		t.Fatalf("failed to decode 429 body: %v", err)
   108→	}
   109→	if body["error"] != "rate_limit_exceeded" {
   110→		t.Errorf("expected error=rate_limit_exceeded, got %v", body["error"])
   111→	}
   112→	if body["retry_after"] == nil {
   113→		t.Error("expected retry_after field in 429 body")
   114→	}
   115→
   116→	// Near-boundary assertion: X-RateLimit-Reset must be a Unix timestamp
   117→	// within [now, now+60] seconds — i.e. within the current 1-minute window.
   118→	resetStr := w.Header().Get("X-RateLimit-Reset")
   119→	if resetStr == "" {
   120→		t.Fatal("expected X-RateLimit-Reset header on 429 response")
   121→	}
   122→	resetTs, err := strconv.ParseInt(resetStr, 10, 64)
   123→	if err != nil {
   124→		t.Fatalf("X-RateLimit-Reset is not a valid int64: %q", resetStr)
   125→	}
   126→	now := time.Now().Unix()
   127→	if resetTs < now || resetTs > now+60 {
   128→		t.Errorf("X-RateLimit-Reset %d is not within [now(%d), now+60(%d)]", resetTs, now, now+60)
   129→	}
   130→}

   132→// TestRateLimitWindowReset verifies that the sliding window allows requests
   133→// again after time has advanced past the window.
   134→func TestRateLimitWindowReset(t *testing.T) {
   135→	rl, mr := newTestRateLimiter(t)
   136→
   137→	const limit = 2
   138→	mw := RateLimitMiddleware(rl, limit, func(c *gin.Context) string {
   139→		return "raven:rl:test:window"
   140→	})
   141→	r := newRateLimitRouter(mw)
   142→
   143→	// Exhaust the limit.
   144→	for i := 0; i < limit; i++ {
   145→		w := doRequest(r)
   146→		if w.Code != http.StatusOK {
   147→			t.Fatalf("request %d should succeed, got %d", i+1, w.Code)
   148→		}
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	// Verify we're now blocked.
   152→	w := doRequest(r)
   153→	if w.Code != http.StatusTooManyRequests {
   154→		t.Fatalf("expected 429, got %d", w.Code)
   155→	}
   156→
   157→	// Fast-forward miniredis clock by 61 seconds so all sorted-set members
   158→	// fall outside the 60-second window.
   159→	mr.FastForward(61 * time.Second)
   160→
   161→	// The window has reset; a new request should succeed.
   162→	w = doRequest(r)
   163→	if w.Code != http.StatusOK {
   164→		t.Fatalf("after window reset expected 200, got %d", w.Code)
   165→	}
   166→}

     6→	"fmt"

     9→	"strconv"
    10→	"sync"
    11→	"time"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
    14→	"github.com/redis/go-redis/v9"
    15→)
    16→
    17→// Context keys used by upstream JWT/auth middleware to store identity values.
    18→const (
    19→	ContextKeyUserID = "user_id"
    20→	ContextKeyOrgID  = "org_id"
    21→	ContextKeyAPIKey = "api_key"
    22→)
    23→
    24→// Valkey key prefixes for each rate-limit scope.
    25→const (
    26→	keyPrefixAPIKey = "raven:rl:apikey:"
    27→	keyPrefixUser   = "raven:rl:user:"
    28→	keyPrefixOrg    = "raven:rl:org:"
    29→)
    30→
    31→// slidingWindowLua is an atomic Lua script that implements a sliding-window
    32→// counter using a Valkey sorted set.
    33→//
    34→// KEYS[1] — the rate-limit key
    35→// ARGV[1] — current time in milliseconds (Unix epoch)
    36→// ARGV[2] — window size in milliseconds
    37→// ARGV[3] — request limit
    38→//
    39→// Returns a three-element array: {current_count, remaining, oldest_ms}.
    40→//   - Admitted:  remaining >= 0  (0 means the last slot was just consumed)
    41→//   - Rejected:  remaining == -1 (count >= limit, request was NOT recorded)
    42→//   - oldest_ms: Unix timestamp in milliseconds of the oldest surviving hit,
    43→//     or 0 if the set is empty. Callers use this to compute the accurate reset
    44→//     time: oldest_ms + window_ms gives the moment the oldest hit expires.
    45→const slidingWindowLua = `
    46→local key    = KEYS[1]
    47→local now    = tonumber(ARGV[1])
    48→local window = tonumber(ARGV[2])
    49→local limit  = tonumber(ARGV[3])
    50→redis.call('ZREMRANGEBYSCORE', key, 0, now - window)
    51→local count = redis.call('ZCARD', key)
    52→local oldest = 0
    53→local oldest_entry = redis.call('ZRANGE', key, 0, 0, 'WITHSCORES')
    54→if #oldest_entry > 0 then
    55→  oldest = tonumber(oldest_entry[2])
    56→end
    57→if count < limit then
    58→  redis.call('ZADD', key, now, now .. math.random())
    59→  redis.call('EXPIRE', key, math.ceil(window/1000) + 1)
    60→  return {count + 1, limit - count - 1, oldest}
    61→end
    62→return {count, -1, oldest}
    63→`
    64→
    65→// RateLimiter holds the Valkey client and logger used by rate-limit middleware.
    66→type RateLimiter struct {
    67→	client redis.Cmdable
    68→	logger *slog.Logger
    69→}
    70→
    71→// NewRateLimiter constructs a RateLimiter from any redis.Cmdable (real client
    72→// or miniredis stub).
    73→func NewRateLimiter(client redis.Cmdable, logger *slog.Logger) *RateLimiter {
    74→	if logger == nil {
    75→		logger = slog.Default()
    76→	}
    77→	return &RateLimiter{client: client, logger: logger}
    78→}
    79→
    80→// rateLimitResult is the decoded response from the Lua script.
    81→type rateLimitResult struct {
    82→	count     int64
    83→	remaining int64
    84→	admitted  bool
    85→	// resetAt is the Unix timestamp (seconds) at which the oldest in-window hit
    86→	// expires, i.e. when the rate-limit counter first drops.  Falls back to
    87→	// now+window when Valkey is unavailable or the window is empty.
    88→	resetAt int64
    89→}
    90→
    91→// redactKey returns a safe representation of a rate-limit key for logging.
    92→// It shows only the first 8 characters followed by "..." to avoid leaking
    93→// user identity (user IDs, org IDs, hashed API keys) into log sinks.
    94→func redactKey(key string) string {
    95→	const maxVisible = 8
    96→	if len(key) <= maxVisible {
    97→		return key
    98→	}
    99→	return key[:maxVisible] + "..."
   100→}
   101→
   102→// check runs the sliding-window Lua script against Valkey and returns the
   103→// result.  On any Valkey error the request is admitted (fail-open) and the
   104→// error is logged as a warning.
   105→func (rl *RateLimiter) check(ctx context.Context, key string, limit int, windowMs int64) rateLimitResult {
   106→	now := time.Now().UnixMilli()
   107→	fallbackResetAt := time.Now().Add(time.Duration(windowMs) * time.Millisecond).Unix()
   108→
   109→	// Apply a short timeout to the Valkey call so a slow/unavailable server
   110→	// does not stall the request indefinitely.
   111→	callCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, 500*time.Millisecond)
   112→	defer cancel()
   113→
   114→	script := redis.NewScript(slidingWindowLua)
   115→	res, err := script.Run(callCtx, rl.client,
   116→		[]string{key},
   117→		strconv.FormatInt(now, 10),
   118→		strconv.FormatInt(windowMs, 10),
   119→		strconv.Itoa(limit),
   120→	).Int64Slice()
   121→
   122→	if err != nil {
   123→		rl.logger.WarnContext(ctx, "rate limiter: valkey unavailable, allowing request",
   124→			slog.String("key", redactKey(key)),
   125→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   126→		)
   127→		// Fail-open: treat as admitted with full remaining quota.
   128→		return rateLimitResult{count: 0, remaining: int64(limit), admitted: true, resetAt: fallbackResetAt}
   129→	}
   130→
   131→	count := res[0]
   132→	remaining := res[1]
   133→	oldestMs := res[2]
   134→
   135→	// Compute the precise reset time: the oldest surviving hit expires at
   136→	// oldest_ms + window_ms.  When the window is empty (oldestMs == 0) fall
   137→	// back to now + window.
   138→	var resetAt int64
   139→	if oldestMs > 0 {
   140→		resetAt = (oldestMs + windowMs) / 1000
   141→	} else {
   142→		resetAt = fallbackResetAt
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	// remaining == -1 is the sentinel the Lua script uses for "rejected".
   146→	admitted := remaining >= 0
   147→	if remaining < 0 {
   148→		remaining = 0 // clamp for header display
   149→	}
   150→	return rateLimitResult{count: count, remaining: remaining, admitted: admitted, resetAt: resetAt}
   151→}
   152→
   153→// fallbackKey builds a rate-limit key from the request's remote address so
   154→// that anonymous or unidentified callers are still rate-limited rather than
   155→// silently bypassing the limiter.
   156→const keyPrefixFallback = "raven:rl:fallback:"
   157→
   158→// fallbackLogSeen deduplicates the "identity lookup missed" warning so it fires
   159→// at most once per IP per 5-minute window rather than on every request.
   160→var fallbackLogSeen sync.Map
   161→
   162→// RateLimitMiddleware is the generic Gin middleware factory.
   163→//
   164→//   - limit    — maximum requests per minute for this scope
   165→//   - keyFn    — extracts the rate-limit key from the request context;
   166→//     returning "" means identity could not be determined; a fallback
   167→//     IP-based key is used instead so the limiter is never silently skipped.
   168→func RateLimitMiddleware(rl *RateLimiter, limit int, keyFn func(*gin.Context) string) gin.HandlerFunc {
   169→	const windowMs = int64(60_000) // 1 minute sliding window
   170→
   171→	return func(c *gin.Context) {
   172→		key := keyFn(c)
   173→		if key == "" {
   174→			// Identity lookup returned no key — fall back to the client IP so
   175→			// the limiter still applies.  Log a warning, but deduplicate per IP
   176→			// to avoid flooding logs under sustained anonymous traffic.
   177→			ip := c.ClientIP()
   178→			const logTTL = 5 * time.Minute
   179→			now := time.Now()
   180→			if v, loaded := fallbackLogSeen.LoadOrStore(ip, now); loaded {
   181→				if now.Sub(v.(time.Time)) >= logTTL {
   182→					// Entry is stale — refresh and log again.
   183→					fallbackLogSeen.Store(ip, now)
   184→					rl.logger.WarnContext(c.Request.Context(),
   185→						"rate limiter: identity lookup missed, using IP fallback",
   186→						slog.String("ip", ip),
   187→						slog.String("path", c.FullPath()),
   188→					)
   189→				}
   190→				// else: recently logged for this IP — stay silent
   191→			} else {
   192→				// First time we've seen this IP fall back — log immediately.
   193→				rl.logger.WarnContext(c.Request.Context(),
   194→					"rate limiter: identity lookup missed, using IP fallback",
   195→					slog.String("ip", ip),
   196→					slog.String("path", c.FullPath()),
   197→				)
   198→			}
   199→			key = keyPrefixFallback + ip
   200→		}
   201→
   202→		result := rl.check(c.Request.Context(), key, limit, windowMs)
   203→
   204→		// Always set informational headers.
   205→		c.Header("X-RateLimit-Limit", strconv.Itoa(limit))
   206→		c.Header("X-RateLimit-Remaining", strconv.FormatInt(result.remaining, 10))
   207→		c.Header("X-RateLimit-Reset", strconv.FormatInt(result.resetAt, 10))
   208→
   209→		if !result.admitted {
   210→			retryAfter := result.resetAt - time.Now().Unix()
   211→			if retryAfter < 1 {
   212→				retryAfter = 1
   213→			}
   214→			c.Header("Retry-After", strconv.FormatInt(retryAfter, 10))
   215→			c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusTooManyRequests, gin.H{
   216→				"error":       "rate_limit_exceeded",
   217→				"retry_after": retryAfter,
   218→			})
   219→			return
   220→		}
   221→
   222→		c.Next()
   223→	}
   224→}
   225→
   226→// apiKeyHash returns the SHA-256 hex digest of an API key, used as the Valkey
   227→// key suffix so that raw secrets are never stored in Valkey.
   228→func apiKeyHash(apiKey string) string {
   229→	h := sha256.Sum256([]byte(apiKey))
   230→	return fmt.Sprintf("%x", h)
   231→}
   232→
   233→// ByAPIKey returns a Gin middleware that rate-limits by API key.
   234→// The raw API key is read from gin.Context key ContextKeyAPIKey and hashed
   235→// with SHA-256 before being used as the Valkey key suffix.
   236→func ByAPIKey(rl *RateLimiter, limit int) gin.HandlerFunc {
   237→	return RateLimitMiddleware(rl, limit, func(c *gin.Context) string {
   238→		raw, ok := c.Get(ContextKeyAPIKey)
   239→		if !ok {
   240→			return ""
   241→		}
   242→		key, ok := raw.(string)
   243→		if !ok || key == "" {
   244→			return ""
   245→		}
   246→		return keyPrefixAPIKey + apiKeyHash(key)
   247→	})
   248→}
   249→
   250→// ByUserID returns a Gin middleware that rate-limits by authenticated user ID.
   251→// The user ID is read from gin.Context key ContextKeyUserID (set by JWT middleware).
   252→func ByUserID(rl *RateLimiter, limit int) gin.HandlerFunc {
   253→	return RateLimitMiddleware(rl, limit, func(c *gin.Context) string {
   254→		raw, ok := c.Get(ContextKeyUserID)
   255→		if !ok {
   256→			return ""
   257→		}
   258→		id, ok := raw.(string)
   259→		if !ok || id == "" {
   260→			return ""
   261→		}
   262→		return keyPrefixUser + id
   263→	})
   264→}
   265→
   266→// ByOrgID returns a Gin middleware that rate-limits by organisation ID.
   267→// The org ID is read from gin.Context key ContextKeyOrgID (set by JWT middleware).
   268→func ByOrgID(rl *RateLimiter, limit int) gin.HandlerFunc {
   269→	return RateLimitMiddleware(rl, limit, func(c *gin.Context) string {
   270→		raw, ok := c.Get(ContextKeyOrgID)
   271→		if !ok {
   272→			return ""
   273→		}
   274→		id, ok := raw.(string)
   275→		if !ok || id == "" {
   276→			return ""
   277→		}
   278→		return keyPrefixOrg + id
   279→	})
   280→}
   281→

  "new_string": "// keyFunc returns the jwt.Keyfunc, refreshing the cache if TTL has elapsed or\n// if forceRefresh is true.\nfunc (c *jwksCache) keyFunc(forceRefresh bool) jwt.Keyfunc {\n\tc.mu.RLock()\n\texpired := time.Since(c.lastRefresh) > jwksCacheTTL\n\tkf := c.keyfunc\n\tc.mu.RUnlock()\n\n\tif forceRefresh || expired {\n\t\t// Refresh in place; log any error but continue with stale keys.\n\t\tif err := c.refresh(); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"[WARN] JWT middleware: JWKS refresh from %s failed: %v — continuing with cached keys\", c.jwksURL, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tc.mu.RLock()\n\t\tkf = c.keyfunc\n\t\tc.mu.RUnlock()\n\t}\n\n\treturn kf.Keyfunc\n}"
}

This typically means Keycloak is not reachable.\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"[WARN] JWT middleware: initial JWKS fetch from %s failed: %v — all protected endpoints will return 503 until the service restarts with a reachable Keycloak instance\", jwksURL, err)\n\t\treturn func(c *gin.Context) {\n\t\t\tc.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusServiceUnavailable, authError{Error: \"jwks_unavailable\"})\n\t\t}\n\t}",

=== PRRT_kwDORyIQwc53Y0xw ===
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**The second limiter hides the first limiter's headers.**

Both middlewares write the same `X-RateLimit-Limit`, `X-RateLimit-Remaining`, and `X-RateLimit-Reset` headers. On successful requests the org middleware overwrites the user middleware, so clients only see org-level remaining/reset even when the user bucket is the next one to reject. If both scopes must be enforced, consolidate them into one middleware that emits the tightest active limit, or expose scope-specific headers.

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```
Verify each finding aga

=== PRRT_kwDORyIQwc53Y0xx ===
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Scope the fallback key by limiter, not just by IP.**

`keyPrefixFallback + ip` is shared by every `RateLimitMiddleware` instance. With `cmd/api/main.go` Lines 71-73 stacking `ByUserID` and `ByOrgID`, a request that misses both identities increments the same sorted set twice, so anonymous/public traffic gets throttled earlier than configured and the later middleware reports headers for a bucket the earlier one already consumed. Give each scope its own fallback namespace, or pass the fallback prefix into `RateLimitMiddleware`.

As per coding guidelines, `inter
