  "new_string": "// RateLimitMiddleware is the generic Gin middleware factory.\n//\n//   - limit          — maximum requests per minute for this scope\n//   - keyFn          — extracts the rate-limit key from the request context;\n//     returning \"\" means identity could not be determined; a fallback\n//     IP-based key is used instead so the limiter is never silently skipped.\n//   - fallbackPrefix — key prefix used when keyFn returns \"\"; each scope\n//     should pass its own prefix so that stacked middlewares do not share\n//     the same anonymous bucket (e.g. keyPrefixUser+\"fallback:\" for ByUserID).\nfunc RateLimitMiddleware(rl *RateLimiter, limit int, keyFn func(*gin.Context) string, fallbackPrefix string) gin.HandlerFunc {\n\tconst windowMs = int64(60_000) // 1 minute sliding window\n\tif fallbackPrefix == \"\" {\n\t\tfallbackPrefix = keyPrefixFallback\n\t}\n\n\treturn func(c *gin.Context) {\n\t\tkey := keyFn(c)\n\t\tif key == \"\" {\n\t\t\t// Identity lookup returned no key — fall back to the client IP so\n\t\t\t// the limiter still applies.  Log a warning, but deduplicate per IP\n\t\t\t// to avoid flooding logs under sustained anonymous traffic.\n\t\t\tip := c.ClientIP()\n\t\t\tconst logTTL = 5 * time.Minute\n\t\t\tnow := time.Now()\n\t\t\tif v, loaded := fallbackLogSeen.LoadOrStore(ip, now); loaded {\n\t\t\t\tif now.Sub(v.(time.Time)) >= logTTL {\n\t\t\t\t\t// Entry is stale — refresh and log again.\n\t\t\t\t\tfallbackLogSeen.Store(ip, now)\n\t\t\t\t\trl.logger.WarnContext(c.Request.Context(),\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\"rate limiter: identity lookup missed, using IP fallback\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"ip\", ip),\n\t\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"path\", c.FullPath()),\n\t\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t// else: recently logged for this IP — stay silent\n\t\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t\t// First time we've seen this IP fall back — log immediately.\n\t\t\t\trl.logger.WarnContext(c.Request.Context(),\n\t\t\t\t\t\"rate limiter: identity lookup missed, using IP fallback\",\n\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"ip\", ip),\n\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"path\", c.FullPath()),\n\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tkey = fallbackPrefix + ip\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tresult := rl.check(c.Request.Context(), key, limit, windowMs)\n\n\t\t// Set X-RateLimit-* headers, keeping the tightest (lowest remaining)\n\t\t// when multiple rate-limit middlewares are stacked on the same route.\n\t\t// This ensures clients always observe the binding constraint.\n\t\tprevRemStr := c.Writer.Header().Get(\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\")\n\t\tif prevRemStr == \"\" {\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"X-RateLimit-Limit\", strconv.Itoa(limit))\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\", strconv.FormatInt(result.remaining, 10))\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"X-RateLimit-Reset\", strconv.FormatInt(result.resetAt, 10))\n\t\t} else if prevRem, err := strconv.ParseInt(prevRemStr, 10, 64); err == nil && result.remaining < prevRem {\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"X-RateLimit-Limit\", strconv.Itoa(limit))\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\", strconv.FormatInt(result.remaining, 10))\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"X-RateLimit-Reset\", strconv.FormatInt(result.resetAt, 10))\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif !result.admitted {\n\t\t\tretryAfter := result.resetAt - time.Now().Unix()\n\t\t\tif retryAfter < 1 {\n\t\t\t\tretryAfter = 1\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"Retry-After\", strconv.FormatInt(retryAfter, 10))\n\t\t\tc.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusTooManyRequests, gin.H{\n\t\t\t\t\"error\":       \"rate_limit_exceeded\",\n\t\t\t\t\"retry_after\": retryAfter,\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tc.Next()\n\t}\n}"

id: toolu_01Rq4C37AXZjyGbUVZTf3Hh1
```json
{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nwith open('internal/middleware/ratelimit_test.go', 'r') as f:\n    content = f.read()\n\nimport re\n\n# Replace each RateLimitMiddleware(rl, ..., func... { ... }) call\n# by adding keyPrefixFallback as the last argument.\n# The pattern: RateLimitMiddleware(rl, <args>, func(c *gin.Context) string {\n#   ...body...\n# })\n# We need to add ', keyPrefixFallback' before the final ')'\n\n# Find all direct calls by tracking from 'RateLimitMiddleware(' to its matching ')'\nresult = []\ni = 0\nwhile i < len(content):\n    match = re.search(r'RateLimitMiddleware\\(', content[i:])\n    if not match:\n        result.append(content[i:])\n        break\n    # Add everything up to and including 'RateLimitMiddleware('\n    start = i + match.start()\n    result.append(content[i:start + len('RateLimitMiddleware(')])\n    \n    # Now find the matching closing ')' by tracking brace/paren depth\n    pos = start + len('RateLimitMiddleware(')\n    depth = 1  # we're inside the outer '('\n    while pos < len(content) and depth > 0:\n        ch = content[pos]\n        if ch == '(':\n            depth += 1\n        elif ch == ')':\n            depth -= 1\n        if depth > 0:\n            pos += 1\n        else:\n            break\n    \n    # content[start + len('RateLimitMiddleware('):pos] is the args\n    args = content[start + len('RateLimitMiddleware('):pos]\n    # Add the fallback prefix before the closing )\n    result.append(args + ', keyPrefixFallback)')\n    i = pos + 1  # skip past the closing )\n\nnew_content = ''.join(result)\nwith open('internal/middleware/ratelimit_test.go', 'w') as f:\n    f.write(new_content)\nprint('done')\n# verify\ncount = new_content.count('RateLimitMiddleware(')\nprint(f'RateLimitMiddleware calls: {count}')\n\""
}

All 4 PRs are now **MERGEABLE** with **0 open threads**. Here's a summary of everything done:

**PR #100** (JWT middleware):
- Added 3-attempt retry with 2s backoff on startup JWKS fetch failure
- Gated `X-API-Key` path behind `cfg.APIKeyEnabled` (disabled by default)
- Logged refresh errors instead of silently discarding them
- Removed overly broad `strings.Contains` matches in `isKeyError`
- Deduplicated API-key stub warning with `sync.Once`
- Added wrong-audience test case; set `APIKeyEnabled: true` in API-key tests
- Resolved merge conflicts with main

**PR #101** (CORS/versioning): Resolved merge conflicts with main

**PR #103** (Rate limiting):
- Added `fallbackPrefix` parameter to `RateLimitMiddleware` so each scope (user/org/apikey) uses its own anonymous fallback bucket
- Headers now expose the tightest remaining limit when middlewares are stacked
- Resolved merge conflicts with main

**PR #104** (CI fixes): Resolved merge conflicts with main

All 4 are ready to squash-merge.

> AGENT

Every check is green across all 4 PRs. Here's the honest summary of what happened:

**What went wrong:** I ran `go test -short ./...` locally, which skips integration tests. The migration tests have `if testing.Short() { t.Skip(...) }` — so they were silently skipped. I should have run the full `go test -race ./...` (matching what CI runs) before committing. That would have caught the `DO $$` dollar-quoting issue immediately.

**The actual bug:** goose splits SQL on `;` by default. Without `-- +goose StatementBegin/StatementEnd` markers, multi-statement blocks like `DO $$ BEGIN ... CREATE ROLE raven_app; ... END $$;` are split at internal semicolons, causing `lib/pq` to receive truncated queries.

**Current state — all 4 PRs:**

package main

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"log"
	"log/slog"
	"net/http"
	"os/signal"
	"syscall"
	"time"

	"github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
	"github.com/redis/go-redis/v9"

	"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/config"
	"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/handler"
	"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/middleware"
	"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/telemetry"
	"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/pkg/apierror"
)

func main() {
	// Load configuration
	cfg, err := config.Load()
	if err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("failed to load config: %v", err)
	}

	// Initialise OpenTelemetry (no-op when disabled or no endpoint).
	otelEndpoint := ""
	if cfg.OTel.Enabled {
		otelEndpoint = cfg.OTel.Endpoint
	}
	otelShutdown, err := telemetry.InitProvider(
		context.Background(),
		cfg.OTel.ServiceName,
		"0.1.0",
		otelEndpoint,
		cfg.Server.Mode,
	)
	if err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("failed to initialise telemetry: %v", err)
	}
	defer func() {
		if err := otelShutdown(context.Background()); err != nil {
			log.Printf("telemetry shutdown error: %v", err)
		}
	}()

	// Set Gin mode
	gin.SetMode(cfg.Server.Mode)

	// Initialise Valkey client for rate limiting.
	valkeyClient := redis.NewClient(&redis.Options{
		Addr: cfg.Valkey.URL,
	})

	// Build rate limiter using config-driven limits.
	rl := middleware.NewRateLimiter(valkeyClient, slog.Default())

	// Create router
	router := gin.Default()

	// Global middleware order: OTel → SecurityHeaders → CORS → ErrorHandler
	router.Use(middleware.OTelMiddleware())
	router.Use(middleware.SecurityHeadersMiddleware())
	router.Use(middleware.CORSMiddleware(&cfg.CORS))
	router.Use(apierror.ErrorHandler())

<<<<<<< HEAD
	// Apply rate limiting by user ID and org ID using config-driven defaults.
	router.Use(middleware.ByUserID(rl, cfg.RateLimit.DefaultUserLimit))
	router.Use(middleware.ByOrgID(rl, cfg.RateLimit.DefaultOrgLimit))

	// Register routes
=======
	// Infrastructure endpoint — intentionally outside the versioned group.
>>>>>>> origin/main
	router.GET("/healthz", handler.HealthCheck)

	// Versioned API group
	v1 := router.Group("/api/v1")
	{
		v1.GET("/ping", handler.Ping)
	}

	// Create HTTP server
	addr := fmt.Sprintf(":%d", cfg.Server.Port)
	srv := &http.Server{
		Addr:    addr,
		Handler: router,
	}

	// Graceful shutdown with signal handling
	ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), syscall.SIGINT, syscall.SIGTERM)
	defer stop()

	// Start server in a goroutine
	go func() {
		log.Printf("Raven API server starting on %s", addr)
		if err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != nil && err != http.ErrServerClosed {
			log.Fatalf("failed to start server: %v", err)
		}
	}()

	// Wait for interrupt signal
	<-ctx.Done()
	log.Println("shutting down server...")

	// Give outstanding requests 5 seconds to complete
	shutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
	defer cancel()

	if err := srv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx); err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("server forced to shutdown: %v", err)
	}

	log.Println("server exited gracefully")
}

struct {\n\tURL string `mapstructure:\"url\"`\n}\n\n// ValkeyConfig holds Valkey (Redis-compatible) connection settings.\ntype ValkeyConfig struct {\n\tURL string `mapstructure:\"url\"`\n}\n\n// GRPCConfig holds gRPC client settings for the AI worker.\ntype GRPCConfig struct {\n\tWorkerAddr string `mapstructure:\"worker_addr\"`\n}\n\n// OTelConfig holds OpenTelemetry settings.\ntype OTelConfig struct {\n\tEndpoint    string `mapstructure:\"endpoint\"`\n\tServiceName string `mapstructure:\"service_name\"`\n\tEnabled     bool   `mapstructure:\"enabled\"`\n}\n\n// Load reads configuration from environment variables and optional config file.\nfunc Load() (*Config, error) {\n\tv := viper.New()\n\n\t// Set defaults\n\tv.SetDefault(\"server.port\", 8080)\n\tv.SetDefault(\"server.mode\", \"debug\")\n\tv.SetDefault(\"grpc.worker_addr\", \"localhost:50051\")\n\tv.SetDefault(\"otel.endpoint\", \"\")\n\tv.SetDefault(\"otel.service_name\", Perfect! Now I have a complete picture. Let me compile my comprehensive report.

---

## Frontend Codebase Analysis - Raven

### 1. TECH STACK

**Framework & Core:**
- **Vue.js 3.5.30** - Modern progressive JavaScript framework
- **Vue Router 5.0.4** - Official router for single-page navigation
- **Pinia 3.0.4** - State management store (Composition API based)
- **TypeScript 5.9.3** - Type-safe JavaScript

**Build & Development:**
- **Vite 8.0.1** - Lightning-fast build tool and dev server
- **@vitejs/plugin-vue 6.0.5** - Vue support for Vite

**Styling:**
- **Tailwind CSS 4.2.2** - Utility-first CSS framework
- **@tailwindcss/vite 4.2.2** - Vite integration for Tailwind

**Code Quality:**
- **ESLint 10.1.0** - Code linting with Vue and TypeScript plugins
- **Prettier 3.8.1** - Code formatter
- **typescript-eslint 8.57.2** - TypeScript-specific linting

**Utilities:**
- **@vueuse/core 14.2.1** - Vue composition utilities

**Deployment:**
- **Cloudflare Pages** (via wrangler.toml configuration)

---

### 2. PAGES/ROUTES

The router is configured with 4 main routes:

| Route | Component | Type | Authentication |
|-------|-----------|------|-----------------|
| `/login` | LoginPage.vue | Auth Form | Public |
| `/dashboard` | DashboardPage.vue | Dashboard | Protected |
| `/` | Root redirect | Redirect to /dashboard | - |
| `/:pathMatch(.*)* ` | NotFoundPage.vue | 404 page | Public |

**Layout Structure:**
- **AuthLayout.vue** - Gradient background with centered card (used for login)
- **DefaultLayout.vue** - Sidebar + Header + Main content area (used for authenticated routes)

**Route Guard:** Implemented via `router.beforeEach()` - redirects unauthenticated users to login with redirect query param

---

### 3. COMPONENTS BUILT

**Structural Components:**
1. **AppHeader.vue** - Top navigation bar
   - Displays "Raven" branding
   - User avatar button with initials
   - Placeholder for user menu dropdown

2. **AppSidebar.vue** - Left navigation sidebar
   - Dark slate-900 background
   - Logo (R in indigo-600)
   - Dashboard link with active state styling
   - Expandable for future navigation items

**Page Components:**
3. **LoginPage.vue** - Authentication form
   - Email and password input fields
   - Form validation (required fields)
   - Tailwind-styled inputs with focus states
   - Note about Keycloak OIDC integration for production

4. **DashboardPage.vue** - Main application dashboard
   - Welcome message
   - 3-card grid layout (Knowledge Bases, Documents, Team Members)
   - All cards marked "Coming soon" with placeholder counts

5. **NotFoundPage.vue** - 404 error page
   - Large 404 display
   - Link back to dashboard

---

### 4. API INTEGRATIONS

**API Client Architecture** (`src/api/client.ts`):
- **Base URL**: `import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || '/api'`
- **Proxy Configuration**: Dev server proxies `/api` requests to `http://localhost:8080`
- **Authentication**: Bearer token in Authorization header (read from `localStorage`)

**API Methods Implemented:**
```typescript
- GET<T>(endpoint, headers?)
- POST<T>(endpoint, body, headers?)
- PUT<T>(endpoint, body, headers?)
- PATCH<T>(endpoint, body, headers?)
- DELETE<T>(endpoint, headers?)
```

**Authentication Flow:**
- Mock login in development (stores mock JWT token)
- Planned: Keycloak OIDC integration for production
- Token storage: localStorage key `auth_token`
- Error handling: JSON error responses with fallback to statusText

**Currently Wired Up:**
- ✅ Basic HTTP client with all CRUD methods
- ✅ Bearer token authentication
- ❌ No actual API calls made (dashboard shows placeholder data)
- ❌ No knowledge base, document, or team member endpoints integrated

---

### 5. STATE MANAGEMENT

**Pinia Store** (`stores/auth.ts`):
- Manages user object, token, and authentication state
- Computed property: `isAuthenticated` (checks user AND token)
- Methods: `setUser()`, `setToken()`, `login()`, `logout()`
- Mock login returns dev user with org/role info

**Composable** (`composables/useAuth.ts`):
- Wrapper around auth store for component usage
- Exposes: `currentUser`, `isAuthenticated`, `login()`, `logout()`

---

### 6. TYPE DEFINITIONS

Well-structured TypeScript interfaces (`types/index.ts`):
```typescript
User {id, email, displayName, orgId, orgRole}
Organization {id, name, slug, status}
Workspace {id, orgId, name, slug}
KnowledgeBase {id, workspaceId, name, description, documentCount}
```

---

### 7. OVERALL COMPLETENESS ASSESSMENT

**COMPLETION LEVEL: ~35-40% (Early Stage MVP)**

**What's Complete:**
✅ Project scaffolding and build setup
✅ Vue 3 + TypeScript configuration
✅ Basic routing with auth guards
✅ Authentication state management (mock)
✅ Login page UI
✅ Main application layout (sidebar + header)
✅ API client boilerplate
✅ Type definitions for domain models
✅ ESLint + Prettier + Tailwind integration

**What's Partially Complete:**
⚠️ Dashboard page (only UI shell, no real data)
⚠️ API integration (client exists but not used)
⚠️ User menu (button exists but no dropdown)

**What's Missing:**
❌ Knowledge base list/detail pages
❌ Document management features
❌ Team member management
❌ Search functionality
❌ Real API integration calls
❌ Error handling/loading states
❌ Keycloak OIDC setup
❌ User settings/profile pages
❌ Workspace/organization switching
❌ Tests (no test framework configured)
❌ Form validation beyond HTML5
❌ Responsive mobile optimization (layout exists but needs testing)

**Key Blockers for Production:**
- No real backend integration (all endpoints)
- Authentication is mock-only
- Dashboard has zero functional content
- No error boundaries or loading states
- No API error handling UI
- Missing critical pages (knowledge bases, documents)

This is a well-architected foundation with proper patterns established (Composition API, type-safe, modern tooling), but the feature implementation is minimal.
